Prop 1: If a person is inside a location and that location is connected to another location through a hallway, then the person can potentially see objects in the other location once they pass through the hallway
Prop 2: If a person is in the Channeling Chamber and another person is in the Side Room, then the first person cannot see the second person
Prop 3: If an object is inside a location and that location is to the right of another location, and a wall is inside the latter location, then the object is to the right of the wall
Prop 4: If a weapon is inside a location and that location is behind another location, and a person is inside the latter location, then the weapon is behind the person
Prop 5: If a person is facing a location and that location contains an object, then the person can see the object

---

------------------------------

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if a person is inside a location and that location is connected to another location through a hallway, then the person can potentially see objects in the other location once they pass through the hallway.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 applies here. According to Prop 1, Ini could potentially see objects or people in the Channeling Chamber while passing through the hallway from the Side Room. Since Ini claims she did not see anyone on the way to the Channeling Chamber, this contradicts the possibility that someone could have been visible from the hallway leading to the Channeling Chamber.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Phoenix states that he shoved Doug Swallow, causing him to fall on top of his umbrella. This implies that Doug fell forward onto the umbrella, which would likely cause the umbrella to be positioned under Doug's body. However, Evidence 2 describes the crime scene where the victim's hand is holding a bottle of medicine labeled as coldkiller, and his watch stopped at 3:05 PM. Additionally, Evidence 2 mentions that the victim is lying head down, which suggests he might have fallen in a way that his head ended up lower than his feet.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can see that Proposition 1 ("If a person is hit from behind then that person falls forward") could potentially apply here, but Phoenix claims he gave Doug a shove, not necessarily from behind. Therefore, we need to consider the spatial arrangement described in the evidence and testimonies.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the propositions directly apply to the contradiction identified.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial description of the crime scene and Phoenix's account of the events. Phoenix's testimony suggests that Doug fell forward onto his umbrella, which would typically mean the umbrella would be underneath Doug's body. However, Evidence 2 indicates that the umbrella is lying open in a distance from the victim, not under him. This spatial inconsistency suggests that Doug did not fall forward onto his umbrella as Phoenix claimed.

------------------------------

However, Atmey also mentions that he looked away from the door for a brief moment (Testimony 0), indicating that he was facing away from the door at some point.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on Atmey's positioning and actions.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1 and 5 are relevant here:
- Prop 1: If a person puts their back to a wall then that person cannot see anyone entering the room.
- Prop 5: If a person is facing away from a door then that person cannot see who enters through the door.

Both propositions suggest that Atmey would not have been able to see the thief entering the room if he was either putting his back to the wall or facing away from the door.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since both Proposition 1 and Proposition 5 apply, we can use them to identify the contradiction. According to Atmey's testimonies, he was initially facing away from the door (Testimony 0) and later put his back to the wall (Testimony 5). Both positions imply that Atmey could not have seen the thief entering the room, which contradicts the idea that he was aware of the thief's presence and actions.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Agent Lang states that the president's body was hidden inside a monster costume ("There's dirt inside the costume. It must have gotten there when the body was hidden inside."). This implies that the body was concealed within the costume, which was likely placed somewhere on the film set.

However, Evidence 5 describes the crime scene notes stating, "Surrounded by shards of concrete. Flecks of something yellow stuck to clothes. Touch the Check Button for details. [caption] The body lying face-up on top of the dirt, and yet there's no dirt on the front of the body." This suggests that the body was directly on the ground and not inside a costume, as there would be dirt on the front of the body if it had been hidden inside a costume and then removed.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to find a spatial proposition that can help us understand the relationship between the body and the costume, and how it relates to the ground or the film set.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1: "If a person is inside a costume and the costume is in front of a location where a body is hidden, then the person is in front of the hidden body."

This proposition does not directly apply because it talks about a person inside a costume in relation to a hidden body, whereas our contradiction involves the body itself being inside the costume and then on the ground.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed propositions directly apply to the contradiction identified. However, we can use basic spatial reasoning to conclude that if the body was hidden inside the costume, it would have come into contact with the dirt inside the costume, resulting in dirt on the front of the body when it was removed. Since the evidence states that there is no dirt on the front of the body, it contradicts the idea that the body was hidden inside the costume.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 5, "testimony": 4}

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since the floor plans and the crime photo suggest that the victim was isolated, the presence of Furio Tigre's fingerprints on the ear medicine contradicts the spatial arrangement of the crime scene.

In this case, we can interpret the snow and lightning as "objects" that could obstruct Butz's view of Dusky Bridge. Given the weather conditions described in Evidence 7, it is plausible that Butz could not clearly see Dusky Bridge from the shack due to the snow and lightning flashes.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

If the prints were upside-down, it suggests that the bottle was held or thrown in a manner inconsistent with a direct forward strike.

However, Evidence 1 contradicts this by stating that when the door to the yard is open, it blocks the view from the yard to the dressing room. This means that even if the door to the dressing room is open, Geiru would not be able to see inside the dressing room from the yard due to the blocking effect of the open door to the yard.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does not directly apply because it assumes the door to the yard does not block the view. Instead, the contradiction arises from the specific spatial arrangement described in Evidence 1, which states that the open door to the yard obstructs the view to the dressing room.

Prop 2: If a person is covered by an object and that object is to the right of another object then the person is to the right of any other person covered by the second object
Prop 3: If a weapon is found in a person's hand and that person is in the same room as another person then the weapon could have been used to attack the other person
Prop 4: If a person is inside a room and that room is disconnected from another room then the person is disconnected from any object inside the other room

- A list of characters, evidences and testimonies

Prop 1: If a person is in the middle of a location and an object is covered by that location, then the person is equidistant from all edges of the object
Prop 2: If a weapon is found in a room and a person blocks the view of that weapon, then the weapon cannot be seen by anyone else in the room
Prop 3: If a person is to the left of a wall and an object is covered by that wall, then the person is to the left of the object
Prop 4: If a person is far from a door and the door is to the right of a weapon, then the person is not close enough to the weapon to have used it
Prop 5: If a person is touching a location and that location is covered by another location, then the person is touching the covering location

---

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 3 applies here: "If a person is to the left of a wall and an object is covered by that wall, then the person is to the left of the object." We can infer that Shih-na was on one side of a wall and the Yatagarasu was on the other side, thus she could not see the Yatagarasu.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Combining this with Shih-na's testimony (Testimony 3), where she says she was in the next room and unable to see the Yatagarasu, we can infer a contradiction. If the Yatagarasu could move through the revolving fireplace wall, it might imply that the wall was not a solid obstruction, and therefore Shih-na should have been able to see the Yatagarasu if it was moving through the wall.

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Rimes Marlon states that at about 10:10 AM, he was in the staff room and heard a loud noise from the orca pool room. He then went to the door to look in and saw coins scattered around but could not see the captain (Jack Shipley) or the orca (Ora Shipley).

From the evidences, Evidence 3 (Whistle) and Evidence 16 (Skull Rock) provide information about the orca pool room. Specifically, the whistle can be used to command the orca, and the skull rock, which was moved from the show pool, was struck by the victim when he fell to his death. 

There is a potential spatial contradiction here because if Rimes was at the door of the orca pool room and could see the coins scattered around, he should have been able to see the larger objects such as the skull rock, especially given its significance and size. However, he claims he could not see the captain or the orca, suggesting visibility issues.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand why Rimes could see the coins but not the larger objects.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If a person is in the staff room and an object is in the orca pool room then the person cannot see the object." This proposition does not directly apply because Rimes was at the door looking into the orca pool room, not in the staff room.

Proposition 2 states: "If a person is facing a wall and an object is behind the wall then the person cannot see the object." This proposition does not apply because there is no mention of walls obstructing Rimes's view.

Proposition 3 states: "If a person is covered by an object and that object is in front of another object then the person is in front of the other object." This proposition does not apply because Rimes is not described as being covered by any object.

Proposition 4 states: "If a person is inside an object and that object is behind a wall then the person is behind the wall." This proposition does not apply because Rimes is not described as being inside any object.

Proposition 5 states: "If a person can see a location and another object is between the person and the location then the person cannot see the location." This proposition applies because if Rimes could see the coins scattered around, but not the captain or the orca, it suggests that there was something between him and those larger objects, which contradicts his ability to see smaller objects like coins.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 5 does apply, indicating that there is a spatial contradiction. If Rimes could see the coins, which are small objects, but not the larger objects like the captain or the orca, it implies that there was something blocking his view of the larger objects but not the smaller ones. This is logically inconsistent because typically, if you can see smaller objects, you should also be able to see larger objects unless there is a specific obstruction.
